# SDS data sources — post-implementation audit (2026-10-12)

Where every field on a generated SDS comes from after the 42 audit items were implemented. The owner-facing versions are the PDFs *SDS-Data-Source-Map-2026-10-12.pdf* (same content, laid out like an SDS) and *SDS-Data-Source-Summary-2026-10-12.pdf* (each field tagged only Generated from real data or Uses fixed text). Findings for review are in [sds-data-sources-findings.md](sds-data-sources-findings.md). Nothing was changed by this audit.

Regenerated after batch F (commit 0c9207b) and the UV nitrile glove change (commit 072cd54): every row describes the current code, and every row is tagged Generated from real data or Uses fixed text. Fixed wording that only prints, or changes, because of the product's data counts as real data.

Layout change after this map (2026-10-19, no change to any data source): Section 11 Carcinogenicity (IARC / NTP / OSHA columns; SDSGenerator::section11 adds carcinogen_rows, carcinogen_intro and carcinogen_dust_note for the automatic listing only, overrides and older snapshots still print the text) and Component Toxicological Data (exposure limits and carcinogen listings per component), and the Section 15 SARA 313 list (De Minimis Threshold column) print as tables (PDFService::renderGridTable, footnote marked "*"). Section 2 prints a "Signal Word:" label; since 2026-10-20 the word itself is plain black text in the same font and size as every other field (no colour or size rule applies to the SDS). PDFService::labelValue keeps a value on its label's line (tab stops every 25 mm from the 50 mm value column). Table cells in Sections 3, 8 and 12 no longer drop their last wrapped line (CELL_SLACK) or overprint across a page break.

## Summary

An SDS is now assembled when it is generated, from your own data. The formula and each raw material's constituents (CAS numbers and percentages), flash points, boiling points, physical states and other properties go through the hazard engine and the transport classifier. These are joined by the CAS determinations (hazard data, exposure limits, element, acrylate and target-organ flags), the product family (UV/LED flag, use text and SDS profile), the regulatory lists you maintain, and the company or private-label manufacturer settings. The engine then chooses standard, translated GHS and OSHA wording to match those results. Sections 2 to 15 agree with one another because they share one classification. A per-product, per-language text edit still replaces any editable line. Every row in this map ends with one of two labels. "Generated from real data" means the line comes from your data, including fixed wording that the data chooses. "Uses fixed text" means the line is the same whatever the data says.

Before the fixes, only about a third of a sheet came from data. Most paragraphs were canned: one fixed first-aid, fire, spill, handling, stability, toxicology, ecology and disposal text, used on nearly every product. Many lines were English only, exact percentages were printed, trade-secret identities leaked in several sections, flammability came from the lowest raw-material flash point, and transport copied one ingredient's DOT row. Now nearly every paragraph is built from hazard codes, physical state, family settings and list matches. Concentrations print as prescribed bands. Trade secrets are masked everywhere. All four languages are fully translated. Fixed text is left only where a regulation or a deliberate owner decision requires it.

**Key points**

- Publishing is blocked on every path and in every language by any of these: a blank emergency phone (the company's, or the manufacturer's on private label), a blank private-label manufacturer name, missing federal hazard data above the threshold, a trade-secret ingredient on the Prop 65 list, an inactive finished good, or a Section 14 result of 'Not determined' or an incomplete transport override. Warnings never block. That covers flash point, STOT organs, combustible dust, physical state, TSCA and composition warnings.
- Enter flash points on raw materials. The product flash point is the weight-averaged value of the raws that have one, so water and other non-flammable raws need a high flash point entered, or the average will understate the product's flash point. If no raw has a flash point, the product is treated as not flammable. Boiling points set Category 1 vs 2 and Packing Group I.
- Keep every raw material's constituent CAS numbers and percentages (min/max) complete. They drive the Section 3 bands, the classification, trade-secret masking, TSCA, SARA 313, HAP, Prop 65 and RCRA. Gaps raise warnings and force TSCA to 'not verified'.
- Maintain the CAS determinations: hazard data, exposure limits (with 'Skin' in the notes where it applies), the nitrogen, sulfur and halogen flags (they decide which combustion gases print), the RCRA metal flags, STOT target organs and route for H370-H373, and the Acrylate flag (it decides which acrylates are named on UV products).
- Mark the Combustible Dust status (Yes or No) on powder and solid raw materials, and set physical states on the product, the family default or the raw materials. A Powder or Solid product that contains a raw marked Yes gets the OSHA Combustible Dust class. A raw left as Not assessed only raises a warning.
- Maintain the regulatory lists (TSCA, SARA 313, HAP, SNUR, Prop 65, carcinogen lists, RCRA waste codes) and the product families. For families, that means the UV/LED flag, the SDS profile (storage range, water-based setting, default physical state, typical pH) and the Recommended Use and Restrictions text in each language. Also keep the disclaimers per language, in Admin Settings and on each manufacturer record.
- Some text stays fixed because a regulation or an owner decision requires it: GHS hazard and precautionary wording (including the official 'Call a POISON CENTER/doctor.'), the prescribed concentration bands and 0.1% cut-off, flammability and transport thresholds, Appendix D headings, standard regulatory sentences, and the UV glove sentence, which is always nitrile.

| Source group | Fields |
|---|---|
| Fields mapped in Sections 1-16 | 312 |
| Printed fields: generated from real data | 254 of 294 (86%) |
| Printed fields: uses fixed text | 40 of 294 (14%) |
| From your product data | 155 (50%) |
| Standard wording chosen by the product's hazards | 80 (26%) |
| Admin settings and per-product edits | 20 (6%) |
| Fixed text (layout, labels, regulatory phrases) | 57 (18%) |
| Conditions that block publishing | 8 |
| Languages | English, Spanish, French, German (same sources; labels and standard wording translated) |

## Section 1: Identification

Section 1 combines the product's code and description from the CMS import (alias sheets: the alias's base code), use text from a per-product override, the product's own text or its active product family (resale sheets: a standard resale sentence), and the supplier block and logo from Admin > Settings or the private-label manufacturer record. All of it is set in fixed, translated labels and page furniture. Batch F (0c9207b, 072cd54) did not change any printed Section 1 line or page furniture. Only the operator-only preview Warnings box gained new messages.

## Section 2: Hazard(s) Identification

Section 2 is built from the formula's hazard classification. The inputs are: ingredient hazard records (PubChem, attributed per class), competent-person determinations, trade-secret entries, mixture calculations, a Flammable Liquids class derived from the product flash point and boiling point, the optional per-product hazard override, the carcinogen list and, since batch F, the Combustible Dust flag on raw materials. The wording comes from fixed GHS phrase and label tables in the sheet language. Two things change that wording: target organs and route entered per CAS fill the H370-H373 statements, and on a UV/LED product family the Other Hazards line prints a polymerization sentence and the glove and eyewear lines print the UV sentences (nitrile gloves always; goggles plus face shield stay for H314/H318). The product family's default physical state now counts when a product has no state of its own. Acrylate flags do not change Section 2. Operator warnings (flash point, STOT organs, combustible dust, physical state) appear only in the preview Warnings box or on the readiness page. The missing-data publish block applies on every publish path.

## Section 3: Composition / Information on Ingredients

Section 3 is built from the formula. Each raw material's ingredients (CAS number, name and percentage) are combined across the formula and any nested finished goods. The hazard engine, the hazard-data records and the competent-person determinations then decide which ingredients are listed and which H-codes they carry, and the rows print in a fixed table whose headings and notes come from the translation files. Every concentration prints as a prescribed band. A trade-secret identity is replaced by its description. A Substance sheet always names its main constituent. Batch F did not change any Section 3 wording; it changed two inputs: the product family's default physical state now decides whether a product without its own Physical State is a liquid (and so whether flammable solvents are listed), and a Powder/Solid product classified only as OSHA Combustible Dust counts as classified, so when no ingredient is listed it prints the classified-but-no-ingredients note instead of the no-hazardous-ingredients note. Operator-only warnings appear in the preview Warnings box and are never printed. A published sheet keeps a frozen copy of this section until a new version is published.

## Section 4: First-Aid Measures

Section 4 is built almost entirely from the product's final GHS health hazard codes. Each route paragraph, the symptoms line and the notes to physician are put together from translated sentences chosen by those codes. Notes to Physician now also adds the official GHS "If exposed or concerned" first-aid phrases (P308+P313 / P308+P311) for CMR and STOT codes. Where target organs are entered for every CAS behind an H370-H373 code, the symptoms line names those organs and the route. Three other things can change Section 4. An optional per-product, per-language text override replaces a whole field, and resale raw materials have their own overrides. On UV-family products an uncured-product sentence is added to Skin Contact, naming the acrylates picked out by the CAS acrylate flag. The symptoms line is also reused in Section 11. Only the banner and the field labels are the same on every sheet.

| Field | Summary | Source type | Comes from | You maintain | If missing, prints | Technical reference |

| SECTION 4: FIRST-AID MEASURES (section banner) | Uses fixed text | translation_or_label_table | Fixed heading text from the translation file for the sheet's language, printed in capitals. Sheet language > English > the raw key name. | Nothing | If the sheet language lacks the text, English prints. If the key is missing in every language, the raw key prints in capitals. Old snapshots without stored strings use the sheet-language translation; 'SECTION' prints only when no file defines the key. | meta.document.section_prefix (SDSDocumentStrings :22, getDocumentStrings :5426) + t('section4.title') SDSGenerator.php:1898; PDFService.php:316-325; preview.php:18,:45 |

## Section 5: Fire-Fighting Measures

Section 5 is standard translated wording. Each paragraph is chosen by the product's final hazard data, the same data Section 2 prints. Flammability has one source. The Flammable Liquids category (Cat 1-4) comes from the product flash point, which is the wt%-weighted average over the raw materials that have one, together with the initial boiling point and the physical state. The physical state is the product's own, else the family default, else the largest raw material's. A finished-good hazard override can replace that category, so Sections 2, 5, 7, 9, 10, 13 and 14 agree. The flash point sentence prints the same string as Section 9: either the formula value rounded to a whole degree and marked '(calculated)', or a kept measured edit exactly as typed. Batch F made the combustion sentence name nitrogen oxides, sulfur oxides or hydrogen halides when a constituent CAS carries that element flag, matching Section 10. It also added two combustible-dust fragments for Powder/Solid products that contain a raw material marked as combustible dust. UV/LED-family products get one extra UV sentence inside Specific Hazards. A per-product override can replace any of the four paragraphs, and the flash point sentence inside a Specific Hazards override is kept current.

## Section 6: Accidental Release Measures

Section 6 is built from standard, translated sentences. The hazard codes, the product flash point, the physical state and the UV family decide which ones print. The aquatic sentences now use the Section 2 hazard statement wording. Operators can replace any of the three fields for one product (or one resale raw material) in one language.

## Section 7: Handling and Storage

Section 7 is built automatically from the product's final GHS hazard codes, the P-statements Section 2 prints, the OSHA Combustible Dust class and the product family. Fixed base sentences come first, then a sentence for each matching hazard class, then UV sentences for products in an active UV/LED family. Storage also adds the family's storage-temperature range and 'Protect from freezing.' for water-based products, and ends by repeating the Section 10 incompatible-materials list. Flammable-liquid wording follows the product flash point (the wt%-weighted average over raws that have one) and the resolved physical state (product > family default > largest raw), so a product with no flash point data gets no flammable-liquid wording. A per-language override in the SDS editor (finished goods and resale raw materials) replaces either paragraph entirely, and a Storage override puts a warning on the preview.

## Section 8: Exposure Controls / Personal Protection

Section 8 lists the federal and CAS-determination exposure limits for each ingredient, with the Section 3 concentration band and trade-secret identities masked. Under the table it names the components whose limit carries a Skin notation. When no limit applies, it prints a fixed "no exposure limits" sentence instead. The engineering-control and PPE sentences are chosen automatically from the product's final hazard codes (flammability comes from the product flash point), its physical state (product > family default > largest raw material) and whether any limit rows exist. On UV-family products, on lines whose tier is hazard-driven (or on all lines when the product has H317), the nitrile glove and UV eyewear sentences replace the tier sentences, and the UV respiratory and clothing sentences are added to theirs. Goggles and a face shield stay for H314/H318. Each line can be replaced by a per-product override.

## Section 9: Physical and Chemical Properties

Section 9 prints every HazCom Appendix D property, then VOC and solids. Most lines come from the finished good, its product family and its raw materials: the product's own fields, the family SDS profile (default physical state, typical pH), the largest raw material, the lowest value, a weighted average or weighted sums. The automatic flash point is rounded to a whole degree and marked "(calculated)". Eleven Appendix D properties have no data field, so they print "Not determined" unless a value is typed for the product. Seventeen lines can be replaced per product and per language. Labels, the title and the fallback wording come from the translation files.

| Field | Summary | Source type | Comes from | You maintain | If missing, prints | Technical reference |

## Section 10: Stability and Reactivity

Section 10 is built mostly from the product's final hazard classification (H-codes), the nitrogen, sulfur and halogen flags on its constituent CAS numbers, and its product family. Flammability has one source, the Flammable Liquids category worked out from the product flash point (wt%-weighted over the raws that have one), the initial boiling point and the physical state, so Section 10 agrees with Sections 2, 5, 7, 9, 13 and 14. A UV/LED family adds the UV-light condition, the hazardous-polymerization Reactivity sentence and the peroxides/initiators incompatible (plus an inhibitor sentence when acrylates are present), and the family SDS profile adds a named storage maximum and, for water-based products, freezing. The translation files supply the wording, and a per-product, per-language override can replace any of the five lines.

## Section 11: Toxicological Information

Section 11 opens with the likely routes of exposure and the same symptoms line as Section 4. Three acute-toxicity route lines follow, then the chronic-effect fragments and the carcinogen and component block. All of this comes from the hazard classification (federal data, CPDs, trade-secret declarations, ATE summation, the Finished good hazard override), the carcinogen registry and the exposure-limit datasets. Since batch F, the STOT statements also name the target organs and route entered on CAS Determinations. Trade-secret identities are masked. Each product can override the Acute Toxicity, Chronic Effects, Carcinogenicity and UV Acrylate Information texts, and the symptoms line follows the Section 4 override.

## Section 12: Ecological Information

Section 12 is built from each constituent's aquatic classifications and M-factors, as summed by the hazard engine or set by a finished-good hazard override, and from the EPA PBT designation on the SARA 313 list. The component table lists only constituents that Section 3 lists, and vendor trade-secret identities are masked. Fixed translation sentences fill anything with no data, and a per-product text override can replace any of the four paragraphs. Batch F did not change Section 12's logic; the only visible change is the French wording of H410-H412.

## Section 13: Disposal Considerations

Section 13 prints three lines. Disposal Methods is a standard sentence. On a UV/LED-family sheet whose RCRA check found nothing and whose composition is complete, a cure-residue sentence is added to it. Typed per-product text replaces the whole Disposal Methods line. Contaminated Packaging is the same fixed sentence on every sheet. The RCRA Hazardous Waste Classification line is built when the sheet is generated, from the sheet's final hazard codes, the formula flash point, the resolved physical state (product > product family default > largest raw material > Liquid), and a match of each component's CAS against the admin-maintained EPA RCRA waste-code list. That match is extended to metal compounds through each CAS's RCRA metals flags.

## Section 14: Transport Information

Section 14 is worked out for each product by the transport classifier. Class 3 follows the product's Flammable Liquids category: the wt%-weighted flash point and lowest boiling point of the raw materials, on a liquid. So Section 14 always agrees with Sections 2, 5, 7, 9, 10 and 13. Classes 8, 6.1 and 9 come from the hazard engine's corrosion, toxicity and aquatic statements. The UN entry depends on the transport product type (or the description) and the physical state. Physical state comes from the product, else its product family's default physical state, else the largest raw material. Special Precautions for User is the standard sentence plus a fragment for each case that applies: Class 3 or Class 8 on the printed hazard class, "Marine pollutant: Yes", or a UV/LED product family. Per-product overrides win line by line. They are entered for each language, resale sheets included, and Special Precautions can now be overridden too. Every language is checked before publishing. Fixed wording (entry names, notes, bulk sentence, fragments, labels) comes from the translation files.

## Section 15: Regulatory Information

Section 15 is built in SDSGenerator::section15(). It checks the product's composition against the regulatory lists you maintain: the TSCA inventory, SARA 313 (with its metal-compound and glycol-ether categories), HAP, SNUR and Prop 65. It also uses the Section 2 hazard result, which now includes OSHA Combustible Dust and the product family's default physical state, plus one per-product editor note (State Regulations) and fixed translated wording. OSHA and TSCA Status are automatic and cannot be edited per product. Trade-secret ingredient identities are masked on every line. Batch F changed two things here: the Prop 65 warning grammar ("is"/"are" by count, cancer clause first) and the English HAP heading, which now uses the Clean Air Act 112(b) title like ES/FR/DE.

## Section 16: Other Information

Section 16 is built entirely from publish metadata (the version number and effective date stamped when a sheet is published), the per-language translation files (labels, draft text, abbreviation master table) and the Admin or manufacturer disclaimer text. Nothing in it can be typed per product. Batch F (0c9207b / 072cd54) changed only the abbreviation tables: SDS (ES HDS, FR FDS), ASTM, ISEA and PBT are now defined wherever they print.

## Document header, footer, disclaimer, metadata and file names

Document chrome is the page-1 header, section banners, footer, disclaimer, PDF properties, file names and the preview page around the PDF. It comes mainly from company or manufacturer settings, the per-language translation files and the version and date stamped at publish, plus a few fixed layout constants. Alias sheets print the alias base code, and senders always use an alias's own published PDF. In this scope, batch F (0c9207b, 072cd54) changed only the preview Warnings box, which gained STOT target-organ, combustible-dust and family physical-state messages. Nothing printed on the PDF chrome changed.

## Calculated values and their inputs

In this scope, every number, range and derived classification on the sheet is worked out when the sheet is generated. The inputs are the formula lines and each raw material's own data: constituent percentages, flash and boiling points, specific gravity, VOC, solubility, physical state and combustible-dust status. Next come the CAS data (classifications, exposure limits, and the element, acrylate and target-organ flags), the product family's UV flag and SDS profile, and the admin regulatory lists. These pass through the hazard engine and the transport classifier. The product flash point is the wt%-weighted average of the raw materials that have one, and a product with no flash point is not flammable. Batch F made these changes. The calculated flash point prints rounded to a whole degree with "(calculated)", and a measured edit prints as typed. A product family can set a default physical state, storage temperatures, water-based status and a typical pH. Section 5 names combustion products from the CAS element flags, and H370-H373 can name target organs. A Powder or Solid product that contains a raw material marked combustible dust gets the OSHA Combustible Dust class and its Section 5 and 7 sentences. Section 8 adds a skin-notation line and a ventilation sentence for products with no exposure limits. Section 13 adds contaminated packaging and a UV cure-residue sentence. Section 14 Special Precautions adds Class 3, Class 8, marine and UV sentences. Missing data raises preview warnings, which are never printed. Per-product text overrides still win where they exist.

| Field | Summary | Source type | Comes from | You maintain | If missing, prints | Technical reference |

Ingredient flammable classes are ignored, and a product whose resolved state (its own, the family default or the largest raw material's) is Solid, Powder or Paste is never classified. | Flash Point (°C) and Boiling Point (°C) on raw materials; Physical State on the product, its family's default, or its raw materials; a Flammable Liquids class in the finished good's hazard override only for a tested product | No flash point on any raw material: not classified as flammable. ## Product families and UV products

In this scope, text comes from the product family resolved for each item. The family supplies its UV/LED flag, its active flag, its per-language Section 1 text and, new in batch F, its SDS profile (storage temperature range, water-based setting, default physical state, typical pH). A product gets its family from a manual pick, a rule or the content vote, in which all UV/LED families count together. The UV/LED flag together with the UV Acrylate Rule Pack switch drives the UV sentences in the Section 2/8 PPE fields, Sections 4, 5 and 6, Section 7 Handling and the Section 11 note. The flag alone drives Section 2 Other Hazards, Section 7 Storage, Section 10, the Section 13 cure-residue sentence and the Section 14 UV fragment. The Acrylate flag on each CAS registry entry supplies the acrylate names in Section 4 and the Section 10 inhibitor sentence. On UV sheets with the pack on, the UV glove and eyewear sentences replace the hazard-tier sentences. Gloves are always nitrile. Eyewear keeps goggles and a face shield for H314/H318. Resale raw-material sheets ignore the family for Section 1 only, and the family name prints nowhere. Combustion products, Skin notation, ventilation, contaminated packaging, STOT organs and combustible dust are not driven by the family; they are covered in the other scopes.

## Per-product overrides and admin settings

Each printed line in this scope is built automatically from formula, raw-material, CAS, hazard-engine, product-family and regulatory-list data. If someone has saved a per-product, per-language SDS text edit for that line (text_overrides; on resale sheets the edit is stored on the raw material), the edit prints instead. Some lines take no edit at all: the composition, exposure-limit and regulatory tables, the skin-notation line, OSHA and TSCA status, the RCRA line, Contaminated Packaging, Transport in Bulk and the Section 14 note. Admin Settings, product families (Section 1 defaults, UV/LED flag and the new SDS profile) and manufacturer records supply the supplier block, logo, disclaimer, footnote and family-driven sentences. This version includes batch F (commits 0c9207b and 072cd54). UV inks always print the nitrile glove sentence, corrosive ones included, and the official P308+P311 poison-centre wording is kept.

## Private label and language behaviour

On a private-label sheet the base product's content in each language is reused. Only the parts that identify the product and supplier are replaced: the product identity comes from the private-label registry, the supplier block, logo and disclaimer come from the manufacturer record, and the version comes from the private-label item. Every other printed string, including the batch F wording (combustion products, skin notation, packaging, special precautions, UV polymerization, family-profile, STOT organ and combustible-dust sentences), comes from the per-language translation files and is chosen by the base product's data. Product data, chemical names and trade-secret descriptions print as entered. A vendor trade-secret identity is masked in every section, in every language.

If no logo is uploaded, none prints. | Admin > Settings > Company Logo (upload PNG/JPG/GIF) | No logo is drawn. ## Publish gates and data dependencies

This scope covers the gates, warnings and eligibility rules that decide whether an SDS can be published. None of them prints on the sheet. They read admin settings, manufacturer records, raw-material data (flash point, boiling point, physical state, combustible dust, constituents, trade-secret flags, review state), CAS determinations (hazard data, STOT target organs), product-family SDS profiles, the TSCA inventory, the Prop 65 list and the per-product Section 14 overrides. Every publish path (manual FG and resale, SDS Update, private label, alias publish for senders, bulk/cron) applies the same blocking gates in every language. Batch F added no blocking gate. It added three operator warnings (STOT target organs incomplete, combustible dust not assessed, product-family physical-state note) and new save checks (Water-Based Threshold, family SDS profile, STOT route). The flash point warnings still classify from the unrounded formula flash point and quote it to one decimal, while the sheet prints it rounded with '(calculated)'; the rounding never crosses a category limit. Warnings appear in previews and on the readiness page; only the TSCA warning is flashed at publish. Summary rule: 'Generated from real data' when the gate or message appears, or changes, because of stored data. 'Uses fixed text' when it behaves the same whatever the SDS data is.

| Field | Summary | Source type | Comes from | You maintain | If missing, prints | Technical reference |

## Data you must keep populated (full list)

| Where | Field | Feeds | If blank |
|---|---|---|---|
| Finished goods | Product code and description (CMS import or FG form) | Product Identifier, footer code, PDF title, file names | A blank description prints the code alone ("BK1080") |
| Raw materials | Supplier product name (resale raw materials) | Product Identifier on resale RM SDSs | Prints the RM base code alone |
| Finished goods | Alias customer code and description (CMS import; the Aliases screen is read-only) | Product Identifier and footer code on alias SDSs (code before the first hyphen) | A blank alias description prints the alias base code alone |
| Manufacturers | Private-label item custom code / custom description (optional) | Product Identifier, footer and file name on private-label SDSs | Falls back to the shared alias code/description, then the product code/description |
| Product families | Recommended Use text in each language (EN, ES, FR, DE) | Section 1 Recommended Use (products only, not resale) | A blank language prints that language's standard sentence, never the family's English text |
| Product families | Restrictions on Use text in each language | Section 1 Restrictions on Use (products only, not resale) | A blank language prints that language's standard sentence, never the family's English text |
| Product families | Family rules, active flag, Recompute now, "Reset legacy manual picks to Auto" | Which family text a product gets (and, for other sections, its UV flag and SDS profile) | With no active family resolved, the standard sentence prints; an inactive family is ignored even when picked manually |
| Product families | SDS profile (default physical state, storage temperatures, water-based, typical pH) | Nothing in Section 1. Sections 6-10 and 14; a family default physical state that differs from the largest raw material adds a note to the preview Warnings box | No family values; Section 1 is unaffected |
| Finished goods | Product's own Recommended Use / Restrictions (keep blank unless product-specific) | Section 1 use text; outranks the family | Blank is correct and lets family text through. If filled, it prints as typed in every language. |
| SDS editor (per product) | Section 1 Recommended Use / Restrictions override per language (FG editor; resale editor per raw material) | Section 1 use text (highest precedence) | Falls through to the product's own text, then the family text (not resale), then the standard sentence |
| Settings | Company Name | Company line, PDF Author | Line omitted, Author becomes "SDS System", preview warning; never saved = config.php placeholder |
| Settings | Address, City, State, ZIP, Country | Address line | Parts skipped; the line is omitted when all are blank; a never-saved part prints the config.php placeholder |
| Settings | Phone | Phone line | Line omitted with a preview warning; publishing not blocked |
| Settings | Email | Email line | Line omitted |
| Settings | Website | Website line | Line omitted |
| Settings | Emergency Phone | Emergency line on standard, resale and alias SDSs | Line omitted and publishing blocked (the settings form refuses a blank value) |
| Settings | Company logo upload | Page 1 header logo | No logo |
| Settings | Time zone | Effective date in the footer and Section 16 | config app.timezone (America/Chicago by default) |
| Manufacturers | Name | Company line, PDF Author and file name on private-label SDSs | The form refuses it; publishing blocked |
| Manufacturers | Address fields, phone, email, website | Supplier block on private-label SDSs | Each blank line is omitted with no fallback to the base company; a blank phone warns in the preview |
| Manufacturers | Emergency phone | Emergency line on private-label SDSs | The form refuses it; private-label publish refused |
| Manufacturers | Logo | Page 1 header on private-label SDSs | No logo (never the base company's) |
| Raw materials | Current hazard data for each ingredient CAS (PubChem hazard refresh / current source record) | Signal word, pictograms, hazard class lines, Hazard Statements list, H-phrases, P-statements, PPE icons and sentences, Not-classified sentence | The engine logs the CAS as 'no data'. At or above the missing-data threshold, every publish path is blocked unless a determination exists. Below it, the ingredient adds nothing and the sheet can print 'Not a hazardous substance or mixture.' |
| CAS determinations | Competent-person determination (classes, H/P codes, pictograms, signal word) for each CAS without federal data | All Section 2 hazard lines. H-codes and pictograms are limited to the classes that trigger; its P-codes and signal word are used as entered. | Publishing is blocked if the CAS is at or above sds.missing_threshold_pct; otherwise the CAS adds nothing |
| CAS determinations | STOT Target Organs tab: target organs and route for single exposure (H370/H371) and repeated exposure (H372/H373) per CAS | Organs and route inside the H370-H373 statements on the class lines and in the Hazard Statements list (also Sections 4 and 11) | The standard statement without organs. Organs on only some sources of a code: standard statement plus a preview warning |
| Raw materials | Constituent CAS numbers and percentages | Which hazard records trigger (cut-offs, summation, ATE, carcinogen 0.1% threshold) and which CAS stand behind each H370-H373 | The constituent is ignored; hazards can be under-reported |
| Raw materials | Trade-secret manual hazard entry (manual_hazard_json) | Signal word, pictograms, classes, H/P codes. This also applies inside finished-good components, and the ATE and aquatic sums use the raw material's real share. | The trade-secret ingredient contributes no hazards |
| Raw materials | Flash point (°C, '>' flag) and initial boiling point; a high flash point for water and similar raws | Product flash point (wt%-weighted average), and from it the Flammable Liquids class line, H224-H227, GHS02 and its P-codes and signal word | The raw is left out of the average. With no flash point on any raw, no Flammable Liquids class prints. A raw with a flammable ingredient and no flash point raises a preview warning. |
| Raw materials | Physical state on every raw material | Bound-product test for carbon black/TiO2 (any raw not Solid/Powder), the combustible-dust 'not assessed' check, and the product state when neither the finished good nor its family has one (highest-wt% raw that has a state) | Counted as bound (not Solid/Powder): carbon black/TiO2 are left out of classification. A product whose state is blank everywhere is treated as a liquid, with a preview warning. |
| Raw materials | Combustible Dust (Not assessed / Yes / No), from the vendor SDS Section 2, on every powder or solid raw material | Combustible Dust class line under Physical Hazards and the Warning signal word on Powder/Solid products | Not assessed: no line; a Powder/Solid product with an unassessed powder/solid raw gets a preview and readiness warning, never a block |
| Finished goods | Physical state | Flammable Liquids class (Solid, Powder or Paste never gets one) and the combustible-dust test (Powder or Solid only) | The product family's default physical state, else the highest-wt% raw material that has one, else 'Liquid' |
| Product families | SDS profile: Default physical state | Product physical state for the Flammable Liquids class and the combustible-dust test when the product has none of its own | The largest raw material's state decides; a family default that differs from it shows a preview note |
| Product families | UV/LED flag on the family | Other Hazards polymerization sentence (flag alone); with the UV rule pack, the UV glove and eyewear sentences that replace the level sentence and the UV text appended to respiratory and clothing | No UV text; Other Hazards prints 'None known.' |
| Regulatory lists | Carcinogen list (agency, classification) per CAS | Carcinogenicity class lines (Category 1A/1B/2), H350/H351, GHS08, signal word, carcinogen P-codes (IARC Group 3 ignored) | No registry line is added; carcinogenicity shows only if the ingredient hazard data has it |
| Finished goods | Hazard override mode and contents (classes, H-codes, P-codes, pictograms, signal word); needs the hazard_override permission | All Section 2 hazard lines; a Flammable Liquids class or H224-H227 replaces the flash-point line; replace mode suppresses the Combustible Dust line; an override H370-H373 keeps the standard statement | The composition-derived result is used. An override class without its H-code prints the code with no phrase. |
| Settings | sds.inhalation_only_cas (Admin Settings) | Carbon black/TiO2 exclusion in bound products and the H351 powder rule | Built-in default 1333-86-4 and 13463-67-7 |
| Settings | sds.block_publish_missing and sds.missing_threshold_pct (Admin Settings) | Missing-hazard-data publish block on every publish path | Block on, threshold 1.0% (a blank or out-of-range stored value also gives 1.0%) |
| Settings | uv_acrylate_rule_pack (Admin Settings) | UV PPE sentences (not the UV Other Hazards sentence) | Enabled |
| Settings | Admin > Pictograms artwork uploads (GHS01-09, PPE-eye/hand/respiratory/skin) | GHS and PPE pictogram images | Shipped default images are used |
| SDS editor (per product) | Section 2 Other Hazards override, per language (per raw material on resale sheets) | Other Hazards line | 'None known.' prints, or the polymerization sentence on a UV/LED family |
| SDS editor (per product) | Section 8 PPE overrides (respiratory, hand, eye, skin), per language | Section 2 PPE sentences when that field is hazard-driven | Sentence for the H-code level, or the UV sentence on UV products |
| Nothing to maintain | Headings, labels, GHS H/P phrase tables, pictogram and PPE captions, Combustible Dust class name and statement, STOT organ and route words | Section banner, labels, phrases, captions | n/a |
| Finished goods | Substance / Mixture | Type value; Substance identity row; whether the Mixture notes print | Auto: "Mixture" unless the formula is one raw material marked Substance |
| Raw materials | Substance / Mixture | Type value on single-raw-material finished goods and on resale sheets | "Mixture" |
| Finished goods | Formula line percentages, and a current formula on every finished good used as a component | Concentration bands, the 0.1 % cut-off, row order and which ingredients are listed | A component finished good with no current formula adds no ingredients (readiness-page warning only) |
| Raw materials | Ingredient CAS number | The CAS Number cell, and whether the ingredient can be listed at all | The ingredient is never listed and the preview shows a Composition warning |
| Raw materials | Ingredient chemical name | The Chemical Name cell, when no Prop 65 or CAS master name exists | Blank name |
| Regulatory lists | Prop 65 list chemical name and CAS master preferred name | The Chemical Name cell (they take priority over the typed name) | The typed ingredient name prints |
| Raw materials | Ingredient exact % or min % + max % | Concentration band (summed bounds), the 0.1 % cut-off and row order | Counts as 0 %; the ingredient is dropped and the preview shows a Composition warning |
| Regulatory lists | Federal hazard data and exposure limits per CAS | Which ingredients are listed; the H-Codes cell | The ingredient is omitted unless a CPD exists. Publishing is blocked at or above the missing-data threshold. |
| CAS determinations | Active competent-person determination (classes and exposure limits) | Which ingredients are listed; the H-Codes cell (used only when there is no federal data) | The ingredient is omitted. Publishing is blocked at or above the threshold. |
| Regulatory lists | Carcinogen list | H350/H351 on rows and the listing of carcinogen-only CAS | No carcinogen code; the CAS may not be listed |
| Raw materials | Physical state | Wet-mixture rules: carbon black / TiO2 lose H351 but keep their exposure limits; other powder-only CAS lose their exposure limits. The largest raw material's state is also the product state when neither the product nor its family sets one. | Counts as not solid/powder, so the wet-mixture rules apply |
| Finished goods | Physical State | Whether the product can be a flammable liquid, and so whether flammable solvents are listed; whether OSHA Combustible Dust can apply (Powder/Solid), which switches the empty-table note | The product family default physical state is used, else the largest raw material's state, else "Liquid" |
| Product families | Default physical state (SDS profile) | Product state for products with no Physical State of their own: Paste, Solid or Powder means no flammable-liquid class, so flammable-only solvents are not listed; Powder/Solid allows Combustible Dust | The largest raw material's state is used, else "Liquid" |
| Raw materials | Flash point (and initial boiling point) | Whether flammable solvents (at least 1 %) are listed as contributors to the product Flammable Liquids class | Product treated as not flammable; a solvent whose only hazard is flammability is not listed unless it has an exposure limit |
| Raw materials | Combustible dust (Yes / No / Not assessed) | On a Powder/Solid product, a raw marked Yes classifies the product as OSHA Combustible Dust; with no listed ingredient, Section 3 then prints the classified-but-no-ingredients note instead of the no-hazardous-ingredients note | Not assessed: no Combustible Dust class. A preview and readiness warning (never printed) appears only when the product is Powder/Solid, no raw is marked Yes, and this raw is itself Powder/Solid |
| Raw materials | Ingredient trade-secret checkbox and trade-secret description | TRADE SECRET masking of the CAS and name in Sections 3, 8, 11, 12, 13 and 15 | The name prints "Trade Secret". If no raw material ticks the CAS, its real CAS prints. |
| Raw materials | Hazardous-with-no-CAS flag + manual hazard classification; trade-secret H-codes on CAS-less trade-secret ingredients | The synthetic trade-secret row and its H-Codes cell | No trade-secret row and the hazards are not counted. A CAS-less trade secret without H-codes triggers the Composition warning. |
| Finished goods | Hazard override (add / replace) | H-Codes cells (replace mode blanks every cell; add-mode codes are never credited to a CAS); replace mode also suppresses Combustible Dust | The engine result is used |
| Settings | Inhalation-only CAS list (sds.inhalation_only_cas) | Which CAS get the wet-mixture H351 removal and are dropped when nothing is left to report | Built-in defaults 1333-86-4 and 13463-67-7 |
| Settings | Trade-secret description pick-list (sds.trade_secret_descriptions) | Choices for the trade-secret Chemical Name | Free text is still saved, and the list grows automatically when a raw material is saved |
| Settings | Block publish on missing data + missing-data threshold % (0.01-100) | Publish gate for CAS with no hazard data and no CPD | Defaults: blocking on, 1.0 %. A blank, zero or out-of-range stored value uses 1.0 %. |
| Raw materials | Constituent CAS numbers and percentages (exact, or min/max) | Every hazard-driven line (Inhalation, Skin, Eye, Ingestion, Symptoms, Notes to Physician and its P308 phrases) and the acrylate names in the UV skin sentence | The mixture is under-classified, so the generic base sentences print and the symptoms line can read 'No significant symptoms…' |
| Finished goods | Formula lines and percentages (including sub-product components) | All hazard-driven Section 4 lines | With no composition there are no hazard codes, so only the generic base text prints |
| CAS determinations | Hazard classification sources and competent-person determinations per CAS | All hazard-driven Section 4 lines | That CAS contributes no hazard codes, so its first-aid sentences are missing. At or above the missing-data threshold, publishing is blocked (see gates). |
| CAS determinations | STOT target organs and route per CAS (STOT Target Organs tab; lists every CAS behind H370-H373) | Organs and route inside the H370-H373 statements in the symptoms line (and Sections 2 and 11) | The standard statement without organs prints. If only some CAS behind a code have organs, the sheet stays generic and the preview warns; publishing is not blocked. |
| CAS determinations | Acrylate flag per CAS (Acrylate box on the CAS Descriptions tab; 'Seed acrylate flags' preview and Apply) | Which components are named in the UV/EB-curable skin sentence | Not determined: the old CAS list or an 'acrylate' name decides for one more release. Unticked (manual no): never named. |
| Raw materials | Manual hazard JSON (hazardous raws with no CAS) and trade-secret H-codes (also on blank-CAS trade-secret constituents) | All hazard-driven Section 4 lines, on finished-good and resale sheets | Hazards from those raws are lost |
| Raw materials | Physical state on each raw material | Inhalation-only CAS rule: if any raw line is not Solid/Powder, carbon black / TiO2 contribute no hazards at all; if every line is Solid/Powder, each adds H351 at >= 0.1 % | A blank state counts as bound (not powder), so those CAS are left out of classification |
| Finished goods | Hazard override mode (additive / replace) and JSON, where used | All hazard-driven Section 4 lines. In 'replace' mode the carcinogen-registry and powder H351 additions are skipped. An override carrying H370-H373 keeps that statement without organs. Resale sheets never use it. | The calculated classification is used |
| Regulatory lists | Carcinogen lists (CarcinogenService registry) | Symptoms line, Delayed group (H350/H351), and the P308+P313 phrase in Notes to Physician | Carcinogenicity from the registry is not restated in Section 4 |
| Settings | sds.inhalation_only_cas (carbon black and TiO2 are used when it is empty) | Which CAS the inhalation-only rule applies to, which changes every hazard-driven line | The built-in default CAS list is used |
| Settings | uv_acrylate_rule_pack | The UV/EB-curable sentence at the end of Skin Contact | A missing row counts as enabled |
| Product families | is_uv flag, active status, and the family rules and manual picks that set the product's family (UV families are pooled by total share) | The UV/EB-curable sentence at the end of Skin Contact | The sentence is not added |
| Raw materials | Constituent chemical name, trade-secret flag and trade-secret description | Acrylate names inside the UV/EB-curable sentence (after the Prop 65 name and the CAS registry name) | A flagged acrylate with no name prints its CAS. Without a flag, an unnamed component off the old CAS list is not detected. A trade secret prints its description or the translated "Trade Secret". |
| SDS editor (per product) | Optional override text for inhalation, skin, eyes, ingestion, symptoms and notes, per language (finished goods at /sds/{id}/edit; resale raw materials at /sds/resale/{rm}/edit) | The matching Section 4 line on this product and on its alias and private-label sheets. The symptoms override also prints in Section 11. | The automatic text prints. Blank rows are ignored and removed on save. 'Reset to automatic' removes a kept override. |
| Nothing to maintain | Translation files en/es/fr/de (section4.*, labels.*, stot.*, ghs_{lang} h_statements) | All Section 4 wording and labels | Falls back to English, then to the raw key |
| Raw materials | Flash point (°C) and the 'greater than' tick on every raw material that has one (a high value for water and similar raw materials) | The product flash point (wt%-weighted average, printed rounded with '(calculated)'). It sets the Flammable Liquids category that chooses the Suitable, Unsuitable, Specific Hazards and Advice text, and it gives the flash point sentence (also used in Sections 2, 7, 9, 10, 13 and 14) | That raw material is left out of the average. If no raw material has a flash point, the product is not flammable: default Section 5 text, no flash point sentence, and Section 9 prints 'Not determined' |
| Raw materials | Initial boiling point (°C) on solvent raw materials | Cat 1 vs Cat 2 when the product flash point is below 23 °C (the lowest raw boiling point is used) | Cat 2 is assumed and the preview shows a warning |
| Finished goods | Physical state | Whether the product is assessed as a liquid (Solid, Powder and Paste are never Flammable Liquids), and whether it can be a combustible dust (Powder or Solid) | Family default physical state > the highest-wt% raw material that has one > 'Liquid' (with a preview warning) |
| Product families | SDS profile: Default physical state (e.g. Paste for offset and UV paste inks) | The physical state used when the finished good has none, and so the flammable-liquid and combustible-dust tiers | The largest raw material's state, else 'Liquid' |
| Raw materials | Combustible Dust (Not assessed / Yes / No) on raw materials, from the vendor SDS Section 2 (needed on every Powder/Solid raw; a raw marked Yes counts whatever its own state) | The OSHA Combustible Dust class, which adds the dust sentence to Unsuitable media and to Specific Hazards on Powder/Solid products | 'Not assessed' adds no dust text; the preview and SDS Creation Readiness warn when the product is Powder/Solid |
| CAS determinations | Nitrogen / sulfur / halogen element flags per CAS (CAS Descriptions tab; set from the chemical name when a new CAS is first saved on a raw material; seed at /determinations/element-flags from the molecular formula, else names) | The combustion products named in Specific Hazards (and the Section 10 decomposition products) | Only carbon monoxide and carbon dioxide are named |
| Raw materials | Constituent CAS numbers and percentages | The hazard codes behind the water-reactive, oxidizer, organic peroxide and explosive tiers, and the element flags in the combustion sentence | No hazard codes or element flags are carried in, and the generic default text prints |
| CAS determinations | Hazard classifications/H-codes per CAS (H260/H261, H271/H272, H240-H242, H200-H205), with a competent-person determination when no federal or vendor data exist | Suitable and Unsuitable media, Specific Hazards sentences, Advice for Firefighters. Ingredient H224-H227 do not set the product's flammability | The default text prints. A CAS at or above the missing-data threshold with no data blocks publishing |
| Raw materials | Manual hazard entries on hazardous no-CAS (trade-secret) raw materials | The same non-flammability tiers (counted at 100%, no cut-off). Their Flammable Liquids codes are ignored | Those ingredients add no fire hazard codes |
| Finished goods | Hazard override H-codes/classes and mode (add / replace), e.g. for a tested flash point category | All Section 5 tiers. A Flammable Liquids class or H224-H227 here replaces the flash-point-derived category. Replace mode also suppresses the combustible-dust class | Calculated codes only |
| SDS editor (per product) | Section 9 Flash Point edit (per language, a measured temperature with unit) | The flash point sentence and Section 9, printed exactly as typed without '(calculated)', and only when it agrees with the classification | The rounded formula value with '(calculated)' is used |
| SDS editor (per product) | Section 5 overrides: Suitable, Unsuitable, Specific Hazards, Firefighter Advice (per language, finished good or resale raw material) | The matching paragraph, which the override replaces in full (the flash point sentence in Specific Hazards stays current) | Automatic text prints |
| Settings | UV acrylate rule pack (uv_acrylate_rule_pack) | Specific Hazards UV/EB sentence | A missing setting counts as enabled |
| Product families | UV/LED flag (is_uv) and Active status on the family; family rules | Specific Hazards UV/EB sentence (CAS acrylate flags do not affect Section 5) | No UV sentence |
| Finished goods | Product family (automatic from formula content, or a manual pick; the raw material's family on a resale sheet) | The Specific Hazards UV/EB sentence and the family default physical state | No UV sentence; no family default state |
| Nothing to maintain | Section heading and field labels | Banner and labels | n/a (translation files) |
| CAS determinations | Current hazard classifications and competent-person determinations for every ingredient CAS: acute toxicity categories and ATE values, skin corrosion, aquatic acute and chronic with M-factors, and pyrophoric, water-reactive, flammable gas or flammable solid codes | Which Personal Precautions paragraph prints, the corrosive add-on, the H220-H223/H228 flammable add-on, the H250/H260/H261 ignition add-on, and every aquatic sentence in Environmental Precautions | Only the standard paragraph and the drains sentence print, so a toxic, corrosive or aquatic-hazard product gets no matching spill advice. | Raw materials | Flash Point (°C) and its '>' flag on every raw material, including a high value for water and similar raws | Product flash point (wt%-weighted over raws that have one), which sets Flammable Liquids Cat 1-4. If none has one, the product counts as not flammable and neither add-on prints. |
| Raw materials | Constituent percentages (exact/min/max) and formula line percentages | Whether each hazard class passes its cut-off or ATEmix, and the weights of the flash point average, so every hazard-driven sentence in Section 6 | Classes can drop out and the matching sentences do not print |
| Raw materials | Trade-secret H-codes on withheld constituents; manual hazard data on hazardous no-CAS raw materials | Personal Precautions paragraph choice, corrosive and ignition add-ons, aquatic sentences (trade-secret flammable-liquid codes are not used; the flash point decides) | Hazards of secret ingredients are ignored in Section 6 |
| Finished goods | Hazard override mode / hazard override (optional) | Hazard codes for every Section 6 hazard sentence on finished-good sheets. A Flammable Liquids class or H224-H227 in the override replaces the flash-point category. | Calculated hazard codes are used |
| Finished goods | Physical State | Containment and Cleanup text (solid/powder, gel/paste, gas or liquid). Solid, Powder or Paste also means no Flammable Liquids category. | The product family's default physical state is used, then the state of the largest raw material that has one |
| Product families | Default physical state (SDS profile; Liquid, Paste, Gel, Solid or Powder) on active families | Containment and Cleanup text and the flammable-liquid check for every product in the family that has no Physical State of its own | '(none: largest raw material decides)': the largest raw material that has a state sets it. An inactive family's default is ignored. |
| Raw materials | Physical State (at least on the largest raw material in each formula; on resale raw materials for their own sheets) | Containment and Cleanup text and the flammability state when neither the finished good nor its family has a state | Raws without a state are skipped. If no raw has one, 'Liquid' is assumed, the dam-and-absorb text prints and the preview shows a warning. |
